<p><b>French Polynesia</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;f&#39; in &#39;find&#39;">f</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/ɛ/: &#39;e&#39; in &#39;dress&#39;">ɛ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/tʃ/: &#39;ch&#39; in &#39;China&#39;">tʃ</span></span><span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;p&#39; in &#39;pie&#39;">p</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="/ʒ/: &#39;s&#39; in &#39;pleasure&#39;">ʒ</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-French_Polynesia.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/86/En-us-French_Polynesia.ogg" class="internal" title="En-us-French Polynesia.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span></span>; <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">Polynésie française</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French">[pɔlinezi fʁɑ̃sɛz]</a></span>; <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_language" title="Tahitian language">Tahitian</a>: <i lang="ty">Pōrīnetia Farāni</i>) is an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_collectivity" title="Overseas collectivity">overseas collectivity</a> of <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a> and its sole <a href="/wiki/Overseas_country_of_France" title="Overseas country of France">overseas country</a>. It comprises 121 geographically dispersed islands and <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atolls</a><sup id="cite_ref-islands_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-islands-9">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> stretching over more than 2,000 kilometres (1,200&#160;mi) in the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">South Pacific Ocean</a>. The total land area of French Polynesia is 3,521 square kilometres (1,359&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-land_area_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-land_area-6">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> with a population of 278,786 (Aug. 2022 census).<sup id="cite_ref-pop_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pop-7">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>French Polynesia is divided into five groups of islands: 
</p>
<ol><li>the <a href="/wiki/Society_Islands" title="Society Islands">Society Islands</a> archipelago, comprising the <a href="/wiki/Windward_Islands_(Society_Islands)" title="Windward Islands (Society Islands)">Windward Islands</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands_(Society_Islands)" title="Leeward Islands (Society Islands)">Leeward Islands</a></li>
<li>the <a href="/wiki/Tuamotus" title="Tuamotus">Tuamotu Archipelago</a></li>
<li>the <a href="/wiki/Gambier_Islands" title="Gambier Islands">Gambier Islands</a></li>
<li>the <a href="/wiki/Marquesas_Islands" title="Marquesas Islands">Marquesas Islands</a></li>
<li>the <a href="/wiki/Austral_Islands" title="Austral Islands">Austral Islands</a>.</li></ol>
<p>Among its 121 islands and atolls, 75 were inhabited at the 2017 census.<sup id="cite_ref-islands_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-islands-9">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>, which is in the Society Islands group, is the most populous island, being home to nearly 69% of the population of French Polynesia as of 2017<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>. <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>, located on Tahiti, is the capital of French Polynesia. Although not an integral part of its territory, <a href="/wiki/Clipperton_Island" title="Clipperton Island">Clipperton Island</a> was administered from French Polynesia until 2007.
</p><p>Hundreds of years after the <a href="/wiki/Polynesia#History_of_the_Polynesian_people" title="Polynesia">Great Polynesian Migration</a>, European explorers began traveling through the region, visiting the islands of French Polynesia on several occasions. Traders and whaling ships also visited. In 1842, the French took over the islands and established a French <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">protectorate</a> that they called <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Établissements français d'Océanie (EFO)</i></span></i> (French Establishments/Settlements of Oceania).
</p><p>In 1946, the <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">EFO</i></span></i> became an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_territory_(France)" title="Overseas territory (France)">overseas territory</a> under the constitution of the <a href="/wiki/French_Fourth_Republic" title="French Fourth Republic">French Fourth Republic</a>, and Polynesians were granted the right to vote through citizenship. In 1957, the <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">EFO</i></span></i> were renamed French Polynesia. In 1983 French Polynesia became a member of the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Community" title="Pacific Community">Pacific Community</a>, a regional development organization. Since 28 March 2003, French Polynesia has been an overseas collectivity of the French Republic under the constitutional revision of article 74, and later gained, with law 2004-192 of 27 February 2004, an administrative autonomy, two symbolic manifestations of which are the title of the <a href="/wiki/President_of_French_Polynesia" title="President of French Polynesia">President of French Polynesia</a> and its additional designation as an <a href="/wiki/Overseas_country_of_France" title="Overseas country of France">overseas country</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:282px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg/280px-Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg" decoding="async" width="280" height="174" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg/420px-Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/df/Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg/560px-Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg 2x" data-file-width="748" data-file-height="464" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Floreal-Bora-Bora.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The French frigate <i><a href="/wiki/Flor%C3%A9al_(F_730)" class="mw-redirect" title="Floréal (F 730)">Floréal</a></i> in November 2002, at anchor in <a href="/wiki/Bora_Bora" title="Bora Bora">Bora Bora</a> lagoon</div></div></div>
<p>Anthropologists and historians believe the Great Polynesian Migration commenced around 1500 BC as <a href="/wiki/Austronesian_peoples" title="Austronesian peoples">Austronesian peoples</a> went on a journey using celestial navigation to find islands in the South Pacific Ocean. The first islands of French Polynesia to be settled were the Marquesas Islands in about 200 BC. The Polynesians later ventured southwest and discovered the Society Islands around AD 300.<sup id="cite_ref-KMLA1797_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KMLA1797-11">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>European encounters began in 1521 when Portuguese explorer <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_Magellan" title="Ferdinand Magellan">Ferdinand Magellan</a>, sailing at the service of the <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Spain" title="Monarchy of Spain">Spanish Crown</a>, sighted <a href="/wiki/Puka-Puka" title="Puka-Puka">Puka-Puka</a> in the <a href="/wiki/%C3%8Eles_Tuamotu-Gambier" title="Îles Tuamotu-Gambier">Tuāmotu-Gambier Archipelago</a>. In 1606 another Spanish expedition under <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Fernandes_de_Queir%C3%B3s" title="Pedro Fernandes de Queirós">Pedro Fernandes de Queirós</a> sailed through Polynesia sighting an inhabited island on 10 February<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> which they called Sagitaria (or Sagittaria), probably the island of <a href="/wiki/Rekareka" title="Rekareka">Rekareka</a> to the southeast of Tahiti.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> In 1722, Dutchman <a href="/wiki/Jakob_Roggeveen" class="mw-redirect" title="Jakob Roggeveen">Jakob Roggeveen</a> while on an expedition sponsored by the Dutch West India Company, charted the location of six islands in the Tuamotu Archipelago and two islands in the Society Islands, one of which was <a href="/wiki/Bora_Bora" title="Bora Bora">Bora Bora</a>.
</p><p>British explorer <a href="/wiki/Samuel_Wallis" title="Samuel Wallis">Samuel Wallis</a> became the first European navigator to visit Tahiti in 1767. French explorer <a href="/wiki/Louis_Antoine_de_Bougainville" title="Louis Antoine de Bougainville">Louis Antoine de Bougainville</a> also visited Tahiti in 1768, while British explorer <a href="/wiki/James_Cook" title="James Cook">James Cook</a> arrived in 1769,<sup id="cite_ref-KMLA1797_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KMLA1797-11">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/1769_transit_of_Venus_observed_from_Tahiti" title="1769 transit of Venus observed from Tahiti">observed the transit of Venus</a>. He would stop in Tahiti again in 1773 during his second voyage to the Pacific, and once more in 1777 during his third and last voyage before being killed in Hawaii.
</p><p>In 1772, the Spanish <a href="/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_Peru" title="Viceroyalty of Peru">Viceroy of Peru</a> Don <a href="/wiki/Manuel_de_Amat_y_Junient" class="mw-redirect" title="Manuel de Amat y Junient">Manuel de Amat</a> ordered a number of expeditions to Tahiti under the command of <a href="/wiki/Domingo_de_Bonechea" title="Domingo de Bonechea">Domingo de Bonechea</a> who was the first European to explore all of the main islands beyond Tahiti.<sup id="cite_ref-Paradise_Past_book_(page_43:_The_De_Boenechea_Expedition)_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Paradise_Past_book_(page_43:_The_De_Boenechea_Expedition)-14">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> A short-lived Spanish settlement was created in 1774,<sup id="cite_ref-KMLA1797_11-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KMLA1797-11">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> and for a time some maps bore the name <i>Isla de Amat</i> after Viceroy Amat.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Christian_mission" title="Christian mission">Christian missions</a> began with Spanish priests who stayed in Tahiti for a year. Protestants from the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a> settled permanently in Polynesia in 1797.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Society_kingdoms.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Society_kingdoms.svg/300px-Society_kingdoms.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="105" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Society_kingdoms.svg/450px-Society_kingdoms.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Society_kingdoms.svg/600px-Society_kingdoms.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="711" data-file-height="250" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Society_kingdoms.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Society Island kingdoms</div></div></div>
<p>King <a href="/wiki/Pomare_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Pomare II">Pōmare II</a> of Tahiti was forced to flee to <a href="/wiki/Moorea" class="mw-redirect" title="Moorea">Mo'orea</a> in 1803<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The reason for this is unclear. (May 2021)">why?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>; he and his subjects were converted to Protestantism in 1812. French Catholic missionaries arrived on Tahiti in 1834; their expulsion in 1836 caused France to send a gunboat in 1838. In 1842, Tahiti and <a href="/wiki/Tahuata" title="Tahuata">Tahuata</a> were declared a <a href="/wiki/Protectorate" title="Protectorate">French protectorate</a>, to allow Catholic missionaries to work undisturbed. The capital of <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeetē</a> was founded in 1843. In 1880, France annexed Tahiti, changing the status from that of a protectorate to that of a <a href="/wiki/French_colonial_empires" class="mw-redirect" title="French colonial empires">colony</a>. The island groups were not officially united until the establishment of the French protectorate in 1889.<sup id="cite_ref-KMLA1889_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KMLA1889-16">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After France declared a protectorate over Tahiti in 1842 and <a href="/wiki/Franco-Tahitian_War" title="Franco-Tahitian War">fought a war with Tahiti (1844–1847)</a>, the British and French signed the <a href="/wiki/Jarnac_Convention" title="Jarnac Convention">Jarnac Convention</a> in 1847, declaring that the kingdoms of <a href="/wiki/Raiatea" title="Raiatea">Raiatea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Huahine" title="Huahine">Huahine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bora_Bora" title="Bora Bora">Bora Bora</a> were to remain independent from both powers and that no single chief was to be allowed to reign over the entire archipelago. France eventually broke the agreement, and the islands were annexed and became a colony in 1888 (eight years after the Windward Islands) after many native resistances and conflicts called the <a href="/wiki/Leewards_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Leewards War">Leewards War</a>, lasting until 1897.<sup id="cite_ref-Craig_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Craig-17">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Matsuda_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Matsuda-18">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 1880s, France claimed the <a href="/wiki/Tuamotu_Archipelago" class="mw-redirect" title="Tuamotu Archipelago">Tuamotu Archipelago</a>, which formerly belonged to the <a href="/wiki/Pomare_Dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="Pomare Dynasty">Pōmare Dynasty</a>, without formally annexing it. Having declared a protectorate over Tahuata in 1842, the French regarded the entire Marquesas Islands as French. In 1885, France appointed a governor and established a general council, thus giving it the proper administration for a colony. The islands of <a href="/wiki/Rimatara" title="Rimatara">Rimatara</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rurutu" title="Rurutu">Rūrutu</a> unsuccessfully lobbied for British protection in 1888, so in 1889 they were annexed by France. Postage stamps were first issued in the colony in 1892. The first official name for the colony was <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Établissements de l'Océanie</i></span> (Establishments in Oceania); in 1903 the general council was changed to an advisory council and the colony's name was changed to <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Établissements Français de l'Océanie</i></span> (French Establishments in Oceania).<sup id="cite_ref-KMLA1918_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KMLA1918-19">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1940, the administration of French Polynesia recognised the <a href="/wiki/Free_French_Forces" class="mw-redirect" title="Free French Forces">Free French Forces</a> and many Polynesians served in World War II. Unknown at the time to the French and Polynesians, the <a href="/wiki/Fumimaro_Konoe" title="Fumimaro Konoe">Konoe</a> Cabinet in <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Japan" class="mw-redirect" title="Imperial Japan">Imperial Japan</a> on 16 September 1940 included French Polynesia among the many territories which were to become <a href="/wiki/Greater_East_Asia_Co-Prosperity_Sphere#Japanese-governed" title="Greater East Asia Co-Prosperity Sphere">Japanese possessions</a>, as part of the "<a href="/wiki/Greater_East_Asia_Co-Prosperity_Sphere" title="Greater East Asia Co-Prosperity Sphere">Eastern Pacific Government-General</a>" in the <a href="/wiki/Hypothetical_Axis_victory_in_World_War_II" title="Hypothetical Axis victory in World War II">post-war world</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> However, in the course of the war in the Pacific the Japanese were not able to launch an actual invasion of the French islands.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_(1943).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_%281943%29.jpg/220px-FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_%281943%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="285" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_%281943%29.jpg/330px-FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_%281943%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_%281943%29.jpg/440px-FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_%281943%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3736" data-file-height="4837" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:FRE-OCE-12-French_Oceania-2_francs_(1943).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A two-franc <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a> emergency-issue banknote (1943), printed in <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>, and depicting the outline of <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a> on the reverse</div></div></div>
<p>In 1946, Polynesians were granted French citizenship and the islands' status was changed to an overseas territory; the islands' name was changed in 1957 to <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Polynésie Française</i></span> (French Polynesia). In 1962, France's early <a href="/wiki/France_and_weapons_of_mass_destruction" title="France and weapons of mass destruction">nuclear testing</a> ground in <a href="/wiki/Algeria" title="Algeria">Algeria</a> was no longer usable when Algeria became independent and the <a href="/wiki/Moruroa" title="Moruroa">Moruroa atoll</a> in the Tuamotu Archipelago was selected as the new testing site; tests were conducted underground after 1974.<sup id="cite_ref-KMLA1977_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KMLA1977-21">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> In 1977, French Polynesia was granted partial internal autonomy; in 1984, the autonomy was extended. French Polynesia became a full overseas collectivity of France in 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-22">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 1995, France stirred up widespread protests by resuming nuclear testing at <a href="/wiki/Fangataufa" title="Fangataufa">Fangataufa</a> atoll after a three-year moratorium. The last test was on 27 January 1996. On 29 January 1996, France announced that it would accede to the <a href="/wiki/Comprehensive_Test_Ban_Treaty" class="mw-redirect" title="Comprehensive Test Ban Treaty">Comprehensive Test Ban Treaty</a>, and no longer test nuclear weapons.<sup id="cite_ref-NYT_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NYT-23">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>French Polynesia was relisted in the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">United Nations list of non-self-governing territories</a> in 2013, making it eligible for a UN-backed independence referendum. The relisting was made after the indigenous opposition was voiced and supported by the Polynesian Leaders Group, Pacific Conference of Churches, Women's International League for Peace and Freedom, Non-Aligned Movement, World Council of Churches, and Melanesian Spearhead Group.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Governance">Governance</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Governance">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_French_Polynesia" title="Politics of French Polynesia">Politics of French Polynesia</a></div>
<p>Under the terms of Article 74 of the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_France" title="Constitution of France">French constitution</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Organic_Law" class="mw-redirect" title="Organic Law">Organic Law</a> 2014–192 on the statute of autonomy of French Polynesia, politics of French Polynesia takes place in a framework of a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> French overseas collectivity, whereby the President of French Polynesia is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>, and of a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a>. <a href="/wiki/Executive_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Executive power">Executive power</a> is exercised by the government. <a href="/wiki/Legislative_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative power">Legislative power</a> is vested in both the government and the <a href="/wiki/Assembly_of_French_Polynesia" title="Assembly of French Polynesia">Assembly of French Polynesia</a> (the territorial assembly).
</p><p>Political life in French Polynesia was marked by great instability from the mid-2000s to the mid-2010s. The anti-independence right-wing president of French Polynesia, <a href="/wiki/Gaston_Flosse" title="Gaston Flosse">Gaston Flosse</a>, who had been in power since 1991, had supported the resumption of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_nuclear_weapons_tests_of_France" title="List of nuclear weapons tests of France">French nuclear weapons tests</a> in 1995, and had obtained from his longtime friend and political ally <a href="/wiki/Jacques_Chirac" title="Jacques Chirac">Jacques Chirac</a>, then president of France, a status of expanded autonomy for French Polynesia in 2004, failed to secure an absolute majority in the <a href="/wiki/2004_French_Polynesian_legislative_election" title="2004 French Polynesian legislative election">2004 French Polynesian legislative election</a>, resulting in deadlock at the <a href="/wiki/Assembly_of_French_Polynesia" title="Assembly of French Polynesia">Assembly of French Polynesia</a>. Flosse's longtime opponent, the pro-independence leader <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Temaru" title="Oscar Temaru">Oscar Temaru</a>, whose pro-independence coalition had won one less seat than Flosse's party in the Assembly, was nonetheless elected president of French Polynesia by the Assembly in June 2004 thanks to the votes of two non-aligned Assembly members. This resulted in several years of political instability, as neither the pro- nor the anti-independence camps were assured of a majority, depending on the votes of smaller non-aligned parties representing the interests of the distant islands of French Polynesia (as opposed to Tahiti). Temaru was toppled from the presidency of French Polynesia in October 2004, succeeded by Flosse who was toppled in March 2005, succeeded by Temaru again who was toppled in December 2006, succeeded by <a href="/wiki/Gaston_Tong_Sang" title="Gaston Tong Sang">Gaston Tong Sang</a>, a close ally of Flosse.
</p><p>On 14 September 2007, the pro-independence leader <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Temaru" title="Oscar Temaru">Oscar Temaru</a> was elected president of French Polynesia for the third time in three years (with 27 of 44 votes cast in the territorial assembly).<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> He replaced former president <a href="/wiki/Gaston_Tong_Sang" title="Gaston Tong Sang">Gaston Tong Sang</a>, opposed to independence, who lost a <a href="/wiki/No-confidence_vote" class="mw-redirect" title="No-confidence vote">no-confidence vote</a> in the Assembly of French Polynesia on 31 August after the longtime former president of French Polynesia, <a href="/wiki/Gaston_Flosse" title="Gaston Flosse">Gaston Flosse</a>, hitherto opposed to independence, sided with his long enemy Oscar Temaru to topple the government of Gaston Tong Sang. Oscar Temaru, however, had no stable majority in the Assembly of French Polynesia, and new territorial elections were held in February 2008 to solve the political crisis.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b7/Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG/220px-Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b7/Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG/330px-Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b7/Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG/440px-Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Assembl%C3%A9e_de_Polyn%C3%A9sie.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Assembly_of_French_Polynesia" title="Assembly of French Polynesia">Assembly of French Polynesia</a></div></div></div>
<p>The party of Gaston Tong Sang won the territorial elections, but that did not solve the political crisis: the two minority parties of Oscar Temaru and Gaston Flosse, who together had one more member in the territorial assembly than the political party of Gaston Tong Sang, allied to prevent Gaston Tong Sang from becoming president of French Polynesia. Gaston Flosse was then elected president of French Polynesia by the territorial assembly on 23 February 2008 with the support of the pro-independence party led by Oscar Temaru, while Oscar Temaru was elected speaker of the territorial assembly with the support of the anti-independence party led by Gaston Flosse. Both formed a coalition cabinet. Many observers doubted that the alliance between the anti-independence Gaston Flosse and the pro-independence Oscar Temaru, designed to prevent Gaston Tong Sang from becoming president of French Polynesia, could last very long.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the <a href="/wiki/2008_French_municipal_elections" title="2008 French municipal elections">French municipal elections held in March 2008</a>, several prominent mayors who were member of the Flosse-Temaru coalition lost their offices in key municipalities of French Polynesia, which was interpreted as a disapproval of the way Gaston Tong Sang, whose party French Polynesian voters had placed first in the territorial elections the month before, had been prevented from becoming president of French Polynesia by the last minute alliance between Flosse and Temaru's parties. Eventually, on 15 April 2008 the government of Gaston Flosse was toppled by a <a href="/wiki/Constructive_vote_of_no_confidence" title="Constructive vote of no confidence">constructive vote of no confidence</a> in the territorial assembly when two members of the Flosse-Temaru coalition left the coalition and sided with Tong Sang's party. Tong Sang's majority in the territorial assembly was very narrow, and he was toppled in February 2009, succeeded by Temaru (supported again by Flosse).
</p><p>Oscar Temaru's return to power was brief as he fell out with Gaston Flosse and was toppled in November 2009, succeeded by Gaston Tong Sang. Tong Sang remained in power for a year and a half before being toppled in a vote of no confidence in April 2011, and succeeded by Temaru. Oscar Temaru's fifth stint as president of French Polynesia lasted two years, during which he campaigned for the re-inscription of French Polynesia on the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">United Nations list of non-self-governing territories</a>. Temaru lost the <a href="/wiki/2013_French_Polynesian_legislative_election" title="2013 French Polynesian legislative election">2013 French Polynesian legislative election</a> by a wide margin, only two weeks before the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> re-registered French Polynesia on its list of non-self governing territories. This was interpreted by political analysts as a rejection by French Polynesian voters of Temaru's push for independence as well as the consequence of the socioeconomic crisis affecting French Polynesia after years of political instability and corruption scandals.
</p><p>Gaston Flosse, whose anti-independence party was the big winner of the 2013 election, succeeded Oscar Temaru as president of French Polynesia in May 2013, but he was removed from office in September 2014 due to a corruption conviction by France's highest court. Flosse was replaced as president of French Polynesia by his second-in-command in the anti-independence camp, <a href="/wiki/%C3%89douard_Fritch" title="Édouard Fritch">Édouard Fritch</a>, who was also Flosse's former son-in-law (divorced from Flosse's daughter). Fritch fell out with Flosse in 2015 as both leaders were vying for control of the anti-independence camp, and Fritch was excluded from Gaston Flosse's party in September 2015, before founding his own anti-independence party, <a href="/wiki/Tapura_Huiraatira" class="mw-redirect" title="Tapura Huiraatira">Tapura Huiraatira</a>, in February 2016. His new party managed to keep a majority in the Assembly of French Polynesia, and Fritch remained president of French Polynesia.
</p><p>Political stability has returned in French Polynesia since the split of the anti-independence camp in 2015–2016. Tapura Huiraatira won 70% of the seats in the Assembly of French Polynesia at the <a href="/wiki/2018_French_Polynesian_legislative_election" title="2018 French Polynesian legislative election">2018 French Polynesian legislative election</a>, defeating both Oscar Temaru's pro-independence party and Gaston Flosse's anti-independence party, and Édouard Fritch was re-elected president of French Polynesia by the Assembly in May 2018. By 2022, Édouard Fritch was the longest-serving president of French Polynesia since Gaston Flosse in the 1990s and early 2000s.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administration">Administration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Administration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bora_Bora,_French_Polynesia.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Bora_Bora%2C_French_Polynesia.jpg/220px-Bora_Bora%2C_French_Polynesia.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Bora_Bora%2C_French_Polynesia.jpg/330px-Bora_Bora%2C_French_Polynesia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Bora_Bora%2C_French_Polynesia.jpg/440px-Bora_Bora%2C_French_Polynesia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bora_Bora,_French_Polynesia.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Bora_Bora" title="Bora Bora">Bora Bora</a></div></div></div>
<p>Between 1946 and 2003, French Polynesia had the status of an overseas territory (<i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Territoire_d%27outre-mer" class="mw-redirect" title="Territoire d&#39;outre-mer">territoire d'outre-mer</a></i></span></i>, or <i>TOM</i>). In 2003, it became an overseas collectivity (<i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Collectivit%C3%A9_d%27outre-mer" class="mw-redirect" title="Collectivité d&#39;outre-mer">collectivité d'outre-mer</a></i></span></i>, or COM). Its statutory law of 27 February 2004 gives it the particular designation of <i><a href="/wiki/Overseas_country_of_France" title="Overseas country of France">overseas country inside the Republic</a></i> (<i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">pays d'outre-mer au sein de la République</i></span></i>, or POM), but without legal modification of its status.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relations_with_mainland_France">Relations with mainland France</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Relations with mainland France">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG/220px-Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG/330px-Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG/440px-Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1063" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Haut_Commissariat_%C3%A0_Papeete.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>High Commission of the <a href="/wiki/French_Fifth_Republic" title="French Fifth Republic">French Fifth Republic</a></div></div></div>
<p>Despite a local assembly and government, French Polynesia is not in a <a href="/wiki/Associated_state" title="Associated state">free association</a> with France, like the <a href="/wiki/Cook_Islands" title="Cook Islands">Cook Islands</a> with <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>. As a French overseas collectivity, the local government has no competence in justice, university education, security and defense. Services in these areas are directly provided and administered by the Government of France, including the <a href="/wiki/National_Gendarmerie" title="National Gendarmerie">National Gendarmerie</a> (which also polices rural and border areas in metropolitan France), and <a href="/wiki/French_Armed_Forces" title="French Armed Forces">French military forces</a>. The collectivity government retains control over primary and secondary education, health, town planning, and the environment.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The highest representative of the State in the territory is the <a href="/wiki/High_Commissioner_of_the_Republic_in_French_Polynesia" class="mw-redirect" title="High Commissioner of the Republic in French Polynesia">High Commissioner of the Republic in French Polynesia</a> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">Haut commissaire de la République en Polynésie française</i>).
</p><p>French Polynesia also sends three <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="Member of Parliament (France)">deputies</a> to the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="National Assembly of France">French National Assembly</a> in three constituencies, <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia%27s_1st_constituency" title="French Polynesia&#39;s 1st constituency">the 1st</a> representing Papeete and its north-eastern suburbs, plus the <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_France" title="Communes of France">commune</a> (municipality) of <a href="/wiki/Moorea-Maiao" title="Moorea-Maiao">Mo'orea-Mai'ao</a>, the <a href="/wiki/%C3%8Eles_Tuamotu-Gambier" title="Îles Tuamotu-Gambier">Tuāmotu-Gambier</a> administrative division, and the Marquesas Islands administrative division, <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia%27s_2nd_constituency" title="French Polynesia&#39;s 2nd constituency">the 2nd</a> representing much of Tahiti outside Papeete and the Austral Islands administrative subdivision, and <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia%27s_3rd_constituency" title="French Polynesia&#39;s 3rd constituency">the 3rd</a> representing the <a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands_(Society_Islands)" title="Leeward Islands (Society Islands)">Leeward Islands</a> <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_French_Polynesia" title="Administrative divisions of French Polynesia">administrative subdivision</a> and the south-western suburbs of Papeete. French Polynesia also sends two senators to the <a href="/wiki/Senate_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Senate of France">French Senate</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Defence">Defence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Defence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The defence of the collectivity is the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/French_Armed_Forces" title="French Armed Forces">French Armed Forces</a>. Some 900 military personnel are deployed in the territory – incorporating the Pacific-Polynesian Marine Infantry Regiment (RIMaP-P)<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-fpolynesia_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fpolynesia-30">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> – along with modest air transport and surveillance assets.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The latter include three <a href="/wiki/Dassault_Falcon_20" title="Dassault Falcon 20">Falcon 200 Gardian maritime surveillance aircraft</a> from <a href="/wiki/French_Naval_Aviation" title="French Naval Aviation">French Naval Aviation</a>, which are to be replaced by the more modern <a href="/wiki/Dassault_Falcon_2000" title="Dassault Falcon 2000">Falcon 2000 Albatros</a> starting in 2025. <sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The former is composed of two <a href="/wiki/CASA/IPTN_CN-235" title="CASA/IPTN CN-235">CN-235</a> tactical transport aircraft drawn from the Air Force's ET 82 "Maine" transport squadron.<sup id="cite_ref-fpolynesia_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fpolynesia-30">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Three principal <a href="/wiki/French_Navy" title="French Navy">French Navy</a> vessels are based in the territory, including: the surveillance frigate <a href="/wiki/French_frigate_Prairial" title="French frigate Prairial"><i>Prairial</i></a>, the patrol and support ship <a href="/wiki/D%27Entrecasteaux-class_patrol_ship" title="D&#39;Entrecasteaux-class patrol ship"><i>Bougainville</i></a> and the coast guard vessel <i>Arago</i>. As of 2021, two smaller port and coastal tugs (RPCs), <i>Maroa</i> and <i>Manini</i>, were also operational in the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Flottille 35F of French naval aviation deploys a detachment of three <a href="/wiki/Eurocopter_AS365_Dauphin" title="Eurocopter AS365 Dauphin">AS 365N Dauphin</a> helicopters in Tahiti.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> The helicopters carry out a variety of roles in the territory or may be embarked on <i>Prairial</i> as required.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> In late 2023 or early 2024, <i>Arago</i> is to be replaced by <i>Teriieroo to Teriierooiterai</i>, a vessel of the new <a href="/wiki/Patrouilleur_Outre-mer" title="Patrouilleur Outre-mer"><i>Félix Éboué</i> class</a> of patrol vessels. The French Navy will further reinforce its offshore patrol capabilities in the region by deploying a second vessel of the class (<i>Philip Bernardino</i>) to Tahiti by 2025.<sup id="cite_ref-NavalNews2021_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NavalNews2021-38">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-second_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-second-39">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/National_Gendarmerie" title="National Gendarmerie">National Gendarmerie</a> deploys some 500 active personnel and civilians, plus around 150 reservists, in French Polynesia.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The patrol boat <i>Jasmin</i> of the <a href="/wiki/Maritime_Gendarmerie" title="Maritime Gendarmerie">Maritime Gendarmerie</a> is also based in the territory and is to be replaced by a new PCG patrol boat in about 2025–2026.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_French_Polynesia" title="Geography of French Polynesia">Geography of French Polynesia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:452px;"><a href="/wiki/File:French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg/450px-French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg.png" decoding="async" width="450" height="450" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg/675px-French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg/900px-French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1260" data-file-height="1260" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:French_Polynesia_relief_map.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of French Polynesia</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg/220px-BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg/330px-BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg/440px-BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BoraBora_SEtienne.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Bora_Bora" title="Bora Bora">Bora Bora</a>, Leeward Islands</div></div></div>
<p>The islands of French Polynesia make up a total land area of 3,521 square kilometres (1,359&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-land_area_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-land_area-6">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> scattered over more than 2,000 kilometres (1,200&#160;mi) of ocean. There are 121 islands in French Polynesia and many more islets or <i>motus</i> around <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atolls</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-islands_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-islands-9">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The highest point is <a href="/wiki/Mount_Orohena" class="mw-redirect" title="Mount Orohena">Mount Orohena</a> on Tahiti.
</p><p>It is made up of five archipelagos. The largest and most populated island is Tahiti, in the Society Islands. 
The archipelagos are:
</p>
<table class="wikitable" style="text-align: center">

</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Aside from Tahiti, some other important atolls, islands, and island groups in French Polynesia are: <a href="/wiki/Ahe" title="Ahe">Ahē</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bora_Bora" title="Bora Bora">Bora Bora</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hiva_Oa" title="Hiva Oa">Hiva 'Oa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Huahine" title="Huahine">Huahine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maiao" title="Maiao">Mai'ao</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maupiti" title="Maupiti">Maupiti</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mehetia" title="Mehetia">Meheti'a</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mo%27orea" title="Mo&#39;orea">Mo'orea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nuku_Hiva" title="Nuku Hiva">Nuku Hiva</a>, <a href="/wiki/Raiatea" title="Raiatea">Raiatea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tahaa" class="mw-redirect" title="Tahaa">Taha'a</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tetiaroa" class="mw-redirect" title="Tetiaroa">Tetiaroa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tubuai_(Austral_Islands)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tubuai (Austral Islands)">Tupua'i</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tupai" title="Tupai">Tūpai</a>.
</p><p>French Polynesia is home to four terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Marquesas_tropical_moist_forests" title="Marquesas tropical moist forests">Marquesas tropical moist forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Society_Islands_tropical_moist_forests" title="Society Islands tropical moist forests">Society Islands tropical moist forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tuamotu_tropical_moist_forests" title="Tuamotu tropical moist forests">Tuamotu tropical moist forests</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tubuai_tropical_moist_forests" title="Tubuai tropical moist forests">Tubuai tropical moist forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-45">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_French_Polynesia" title="Administrative divisions of French Polynesia">Administrative divisions of French Polynesia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg/300px-French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="300" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg/450px-French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg/600px-French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1260" data-file-height="1260" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:French_Polynesia_relief_map_with_communes.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The 5 administrative subdivisions and 48 <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_France" title="Communes of France">communes</a> of French Polynesia.</div></div></div>
<p>French Polynesia is divided in five administrative subdivisions (<i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">subdivisions administratives</i></span></i>):
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Marquesas_Islands" title="Marquesas Islands">Marquesas Islands</a> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">les îles Marquises</i> or officially <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">la subdivision administrative des îles Marquises</i></span></i>)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Leeward_Islands_(Society_Islands)" title="Leeward Islands (Society Islands)">Leeward Islands</a> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">les îles Sous-le-Vent</i> or officially <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">la subdivision administrative des îles Sous-le-Vent</i></span></i>) (the two <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">subdivisions administratives</i></span></i> Windward Islands and Leeward Islands are part of the Society Islands)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Windward_Islands_(Society_Islands)" title="Windward Islands (Society Islands)">Windward Islands</a> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">les îles du Vent</i> or officially <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">la subdivision administrative des îles du Vent</i></span></i>) (the two <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">subdivisions administratives</i></span></i> Windward Islands and Leeward Islands are part of the Society Islands)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/%C3%8Eles_Tuamotu-Gambier" title="Îles Tuamotu-Gambier">Tuāmotu-Gambier</a> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">les Îles Tuamotu-Gambier</i> or officially <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">la subdivision administrative des îles Tuamotu-Gambier</i></span></i>) (the Tuamotus and the Gambier Islands)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Austral_Islands" title="Austral Islands">Austral Islands</a> (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">les îles Australes</i> or officially <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">la subdivision administrative des îles Australes</i></span></i>) (including the <a href="/wiki/Bass_Islands_(French_Polynesia)" title="Bass Islands (French Polynesia)">Bass Islands</a>)</li></ul>
<p>The five administrative subdivisions are not local councils; they are solely <a href="/wiki/Decentralisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Decentralisation">deconcentrated</a> subdivisions of the French central State. At the head of each administrative subdivision is an <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">administrateur d'État</i></span></i> ("State administrator"), generally simply known as <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">administrateur</i></span></i>, also sometimes called <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">chef de la subdivision administrative</i></span></i> ("head of the administrative subdivision"). The <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">administrateur</i></span></i> is a civil servant under the authority of the High Commissioner of the French Republic in French Polynesia in <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a>.
</p><p>Four administrative subdivisions (Marquesas Islands, Leeward Islands, Tuamotu-Gambier, and Austral Islands) each also form a deconcentrated subdivision of the government of French Polynesia. These are called <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">circonscriptions</i></span></i> ("districts"). The head of a <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">circonscription</i></span></i> is the <i><span title="Tahitian-language text"><i lang="ty">tavana hau</i></span></i>, known as <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">administrateur territorial</i></span></i> in French ("territorial administrator"), but the <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_language" title="Tahitian language">Tahitian</a> title <i><span title="Tahitian-language text"><i lang="ty">tavana hau</i></span></i> is most often used. The <i><span title="Tahitian-language text"><i lang="ty">tavana hau</i></span></i> is the direct representative of the <a href="/wiki/President_of_French_Polynesia" title="President of French Polynesia">president of French Polynesia's government</a> who appoints him or her. The Windward Islands, due to their proximity to Papeete, do not form a deconcentrated subdivision of the government of French Polynesia.
</p><p>The 5 administrative subdivisions are themselves divided in 48 <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_France" title="Communes of France">communes</a>. Like all other communes in the French Republic, these are municipalities in which local residents with either a French or another <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">EU</a> citizenship elect a municipal council and a mayor in charge of managing local affairs within the commune. Municipal elections occur every six years on the same date as in the rest of the French Republic (the last municipal elections <a href="/wiki/2020_French_municipal_elections" title="2020 French municipal elections">took place in 2020</a>).
</p>
<table class="wikitable">
<caption>Top three largest communes
</caption>
<tbody><tr>
<th>Commune</th>
<th>Island</th>
<th>Population<br />(2022)
</th></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Faaa" class="mw-redirect" title="Faaa">Faaa</a></td>
<td><a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a></td>
<td>29,826
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Punaauia" title="Punaauia">Punaauia</a></td>
<td><a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a></td>
<td>28,781
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a></td>
<td><a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a></td>
<td>26,654
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>30 communes are further subdivided in 98 <a href="/wiki/Associated_communes_of_France" title="Associated communes of France">associated communes</a> which have each a delegate mayor and a registry office. These 30 communes were subdivided in associated communes either because they have a large land territory (particularly in the larger islands such as <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a> or <a href="/wiki/Nuku_Hiva" title="Nuku Hiva">Nuku Hiva</a>) or because they are made up of <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atolls</a> distant from each other (particularly in the Tuamotu archipelago), which led to the creation of associated communes for each inhabited atoll.
</p><p>17 communes (out of French Polynesia's 48 communes) have banded together in three separate <a href="/wiki/Communaut%C3%A9_de_communes" title="Communauté de communes">communities of communes</a>. These indirectly elected <a href="/wiki/Communes_of_France#Intercommunality" title="Communes of France">intercommunal councils</a> are still relatively new in French Polynesia, and unlike in <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">metropolitan France</a> and its <a href="/wiki/Overseas_departments_and_regions_of_France" title="Overseas departments and regions of France">overseas regions</a> it is not mandatory for the communes in French Polynesia to join an intercommunal council. The three intercommunal councils in existence as of 2022, all formed on a voluntary basis, were:
</p>
<ul><li>community of communes of the Marquesas Islands (in French: <i>communauté de communes des îles Marquises</i>, or CODIM), formed in 2010 by all the communes in the administrative subdivision of the Marquesas Islands</li>
<li>community of communes Hava'i (in French: <i>communauté de communes Hava'i</i>, or CCH), formed in 2012 by all the communes in the administrative subdivision of the Leeward Islands, with the exception of <a href="/wiki/Bora-Bora_(commune)" title="Bora-Bora (commune)">Bora-Bora</a> which preferred to remain separate for financial reasons</li>
<li>community of communes Terehēamanu (in French: <i>communauté de communes Terehēamanu</i>), formed in 2021 by 5 <a href="/wiki/Exurb" title="Exurb">exurban</a> and rural communes on the eastern side of the island of <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>: <a href="/wiki/Hitiaa_O_Te_Ra" title="Hitiaa O Te Ra">Hitiaa O Te Ra</a>, <a href="/wiki/Taiarapu-Est" title="Taiarapu-Est">Taiarapu-Est</a>, <a href="/wiki/Taiarapu-Ouest" title="Taiarapu-Ouest">Taiarapu-Ouest</a>, <a href="/wiki/Teva_I_Uta" title="Teva I Uta">Teva I Uta</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Papara" title="Papara">Papara</a>.</li></ul>
<p>These communities of communes, as elsewhere in the French Republic, are not full-fledged <a href="/wiki/Territorial_collectivity" title="Territorial collectivity">territorial collectivities</a>, but only federations of communes. From a legal standpoint, the only territorial collectivities in French Polynesia are the overseas collectivity of French Polynesia and the 48 communes.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg/220px-Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="180" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg/330px-Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/42/Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg/440px-Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg 2x" data-file-width="626" data-file-height="512" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tahitiennes_en_robe_mission.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tahitian girls, c. 1860–1879</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_French_Polynesia" title="Demographics of French Polynesia">Demographics of French Polynesia</a></div>
<p>Total population was 278,786 according to the August 18, 2022 census,<sup id="cite_ref-pop_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pop-7">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> 68.7% of whom lived on the island of <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a> alone. The urban area of Papeete, the capital city, has 136,771 inhabitants (2017 census).<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the 2017 census, 89.0% of people living in French Polynesia had been born there (up from 87.3% in 2007); 8.1% had been born in <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">Metropolitan France</a> (down from 9.3% in 2007); 1.2% were born elsewhere in <a href="/wiki/Overseas_France" title="Overseas France">overseas France</a> (down from 1.4% in 2007); and 1.7% were from foreign countries (down from 2.0% in 2007).<sup id="cite_ref-Census_2017_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census_2017-47">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> The population of natives of Metropolitan France living in French Polynesia has declined in relative terms since the 1980s, but in absolute terms their population peaked at the 2007 census, when 24,265 lived in French Polynesia (not counting their children born there).<sup id="cite_ref-migration_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-migration-48">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> With the local economic crisis, their population declined to 22,278 at the 2012 census,<sup id="cite_ref-migration_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-migration-48">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> and 22,387 at the 2017 census.<sup id="cite_ref-Census_2017_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census_2017-47">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<table width="720" style="border: 2px solid #999" rules="all" cellspacing="0" cellpadding="4">

</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>At the 1988 census, the last census which asked questions regarding ethnicity, 66.5% of people were ethnically unmixed <a href="/wiki/Polynesia#Cultures_of_Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesians</a>, 7.1% were ethnically Polynesians with light European and/or East Asian mixing, 11.9% were <a href="/wiki/European_ethnic_groups" class="mw-redirect" title="European ethnic groups">Europeans</a> (mostly <a href="/wiki/French_people" title="French people">French</a>), 9.3% were people of mixed European and Polynesian descent, the so-called Demis (literally meaning "Half"), and 4.7% were <a href="/wiki/East_Asians" class="mw-redirect" title="East Asians">East Asians</a> (mainly <a href="/wiki/Overseas_Chinese" title="Overseas Chinese">Chinese</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-ethnicities_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ethnicities-5">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Chinese, Demis, and the white populace are essentially concentrated on the island of Tahiti, particularly in the urban area of Papeete, where their share of the population is thus much greater than in French Polynesia overall.<sup id="cite_ref-ethnicities_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ethnicities-5">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Despite a long history of ethnic mixing, ethnic tensions have been growing in recent years, with politicians using a <a href="/wiki/Xenophobia" title="Xenophobia">xenophobic</a> discourse and fanning the flame of nationalism.<sup id="cite_ref-logiques_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-logiques-49">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:darkblue; width:1%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>

<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;">Chinese</td>
