<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;">Other</td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:purple; width:0.4%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">0.4%</td>
</tr>

</tbody></table>
</div>
</div>
<p>All the indigenous languages of French Polynesia are <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_languages" title="Polynesian languages">Polynesian</a>. French Polynesia has been linguistically diverse since ancient times, with each community having its own local speech variety. These dialects can be grouped into seven languages on the basis of <a href="/wiki/Mutual_intelligibility" title="Mutual intelligibility">mutual intelligibility</a>: <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_language" title="Tahitian language">Tahitian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tuamotuan_language" title="Tuamotuan language">Tuamotuan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rapa_language" title="Rapa language">Rapa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Austral_language" title="Austral language">Austral</a>, <a href="/wiki/North_Marquesan" class="mw-redirect" title="North Marquesan">North Marquesan</a>, <a href="/wiki/South_Marquesan" class="mw-redirect" title="South Marquesan">South Marquesan</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Mangareva_language" title="Mangareva language">Mangarevan</a>. Some of these, especially Tuamotuan, are really <a href="/wiki/Dialect_continua" class="mw-redirect" title="Dialect continua">dialect continua</a> formed by a patchwork of different dialects. The distinction between languages and dialects is notoriously difficult to establish, and so some authors may view two varieties as dialects of the same language, while others may view them as distinct languages. In this way, North and South Marquesan are often grouped together as a single Marquesan language, and Rapa is often viewed as part of Austral subfamily. At the same time, Ra'ivavae is often viewed as distinct from them.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECharpentierFrançois201573–76_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECharpentierFrançois201573–76-55">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a> is the sole official language of French Polynesia.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> An <a href="/wiki/Organic_law" title="Organic law">organic law</a> of 12 April 1996 states that "French is the official language, Tahitian and other Polynesian languages can be used." At the 2017 census, among the population whose age was 15 and older, 73.9% of people reported that the language they spoke the most at home was French (up from 68.6% at the 2007 census), 20.2% reported that the language they spoke the most at home was <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_language" title="Tahitian language">Tahitian</a> (down from 24.3% at the 2007 census), 2.6% reported <a href="/wiki/Marquesan_language" title="Marquesan language">Marquesan</a> and 0.2% the related <a href="/wiki/Mangareva_language" title="Mangareva language">Mangareva language</a> (same percentages for both at the 2007 census), 1.2% reported any of the <a href="/wiki/Austral_language" title="Austral language">Austral languages</a> (down from 1.3% at the 2007 census), 1.0% reported <a href="/wiki/Tuamotuan_language" title="Tuamotuan language">Tuamotuan</a> (down from 1.5% at the 2007 census), 0.6% reported a <a href="/wiki/Varieties_of_Chinese" title="Varieties of Chinese">Chinese dialect</a> (41% of which was <a href="/wiki/Hakka_Chinese" title="Hakka Chinese">Hakka</a>) (down from 1.0% at the 2007 census), and 0.4% another language (more than half of which was <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a>) (down from 0.5% at the 2007 census).<sup id="cite_ref-languages_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-languages-57">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the same census, 95.2% of people whose age was 15 or older reported that they could speak, read and write French (up from 94.7% at the 2007 census), whereas only 1.3% reported that they had no knowledge of French (down from 2.0% at the 2007 census).<sup id="cite_ref-languages_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-languages-57">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> 86.5% of people whose age was 15 or older reported that they had some form of knowledge of at least one Polynesian language (up from 86.4% at the 2007 census but down from 87.8% at the 2012 census), whereas 13.5% reported that they had no knowledge of any of the Polynesian languages (down from 13.6% at the 2007 census but up from 12.2% at the 2012 census).<sup id="cite_ref-languages_57-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-languages-57">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_French_Polynesia" title="Music of French Polynesia">Music of French Polynesia</a></div>
<p>French Polynesia appeared in the <a href="/wiki/World_music" title="World music">world music</a> scene in 1992, recorded by French musicologist <a href="/w/index.php?title=Pascal_Nabet-Meyer&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Pascal Nabet-Meyer (page does not exist)">Pascal Nabet-Meyer</a> with the release of <a href="/wiki/The_Tahitian_Choir" title="The Tahitian Choir">The Tahitian Choir</a>'s recordings of unaccompanied vocal Christian music called <a href="/w/index.php?title=Himene_t%C4%81rava&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Himene tārava (page does not exist)">himene tārava</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> This form of singing is common in French Polynesia and the Cook Islands, and is notable for a unique drop in pitch at the end of the phrases, a characteristic formed by several different voices, accompanied by a steady grunting of staccato, <a href="/wiki/Non-lexical_vocables_in_music" title="Non-lexical vocables in music">nonlexical</a> syllables.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:TuamotuCemetery.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/TuamotuCemetery.jpg/220px-TuamotuCemetery.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/TuamotuCemetery.jpg/330px-TuamotuCemetery.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/TuamotuCemetery.jpg/440px-TuamotuCemetery.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="960" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:TuamotuCemetery.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cemetery in the <a href="/wiki/%C3%8Eles_Tuamotu-Gambier" title="Îles Tuamotu-Gambier">Tuāmotu</a></div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> is the main religion of the islands. A majority of 54% belongs to various <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestant</a> churches, especially the <a href="/wiki/Maohi_Protestant_Church" title="Maohi Protestant Church">Maohi Protestant Church</a>, which is the largest and accounts for more than 50% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-tahitipresse_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tahitipresse-60">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> It traces its origins to <a href="/wiki/P%C5%8Dmare_II" title="Pōmare II">Pōmare II</a>, the king of Tahiti, who converted from traditional beliefs to the <a href="/wiki/Reformed_tradition" class="mw-redirect" title="Reformed tradition">Reformed tradition</a> brought to the islands by the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a>.
</p><p>Latin Rite <a href="/wiki/Catholics" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholics">Catholics</a> constitute a large minority of 38.3%<sup id="cite_ref-:0_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-61">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> of the population (2019)<sup id="cite_ref-:0_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-61">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> which has its own <a href="/wiki/Ecclesiastical_province" title="Ecclesiastical province">ecclesiastical province</a>, comprising the Metropolitan <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Papeete" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Papeete">Archdiocese of Papeete</a> and its only <a href="/wiki/Suffragan" class="mw-redirect" title="Suffragan">suffragan</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Taiohae" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Taiohae">Diocese of Taiohae</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The number and proportion of Catholics has increased significantly since 1950, when they represented 21.6% of the total population.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_61-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-61">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Data from 1991 revealed that Catholics were in the majority in the <a href="/wiki/Tuamotu_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Tuamotu Islands">Tuamotu Islands</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-:1_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Gambier_Islands" title="Gambier Islands">Gambier Islands</a><sup id="cite_ref-:1_63-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Marquesas_Islands" title="Marquesas Islands">Marquesas Islands</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-:1_63-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> while Protestants formed the majority in the <a href="/wiki/Austral_Islands" title="Austral Islands">Austral Islands</a><sup id="cite_ref-:1_63-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> and several of the <a href="/wiki/Society_Islands" title="Society Islands">Society Islands</a> such as Tahiti.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_63-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> This diversity is due to the fact that Protestant missionaries (from <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">England</a> and the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>) first came to one group of islands, and after French colonisation the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> spread to several more scattered islands, but also to the main island of Tahiti.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_63-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-63">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints_in_French_Polynesia" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints in French Polynesia">The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</a> had 28,147 members as of 2018<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Community_of_Christ" title="Community of Christ">Community of Christ</a>, another denomination within the <a href="/wiki/Latter_Day_Saint_movement" title="Latter Day Saint movement">Latter-Day Saint</a> tradition, claimed 9,256 total French Polynesian members as of 2018<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> including Mareva Arnaud Tchong who serves in the church's governing <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Twelve_Apostles_(Community_of_Christ)" title="Council of Twelve Apostles (Community of Christ)">Council of Twelve Apostles</a>. There were about 3,000 <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a> in Tahiti as of 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>,<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> and an estimated 500 Muslims in French Polynesia.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Due to the island location and the fact that the French Polynesia produce a significant array of fruits and vegetables, natural local produce, especially coconut, features in many of the dishes of the islands as does fresh seafood.
foods like <a href="/wiki/Faraoa_%27ipo" title="Faraoa &#39;ipo">Faraoa 'ipo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Poisson_cru" class="mw-redirect" title="Poisson cru">Poisson cru</a> and <a href="/wiki/R%C4%93ti%27a" title="Rēti&#39;a">Rēti'a</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg/220px-Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg/330px-Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg/440px-Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hawaiki_Nui_Va%27a_4.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Va'a (traditional Polynesian outrigger canoe) during the Hawaiki Nui Va'a race</div></div></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Football">Football</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Football">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Football_in_Tahiti" class="mw-redirect" title="Football in Tahiti">Football in Tahiti</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Sport" title="Sport">sport</a> of <a href="/wiki/Football_(soccer)" class="mw-redirect" title="Football (soccer)">football</a> in the island of <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a> is run by the <a href="/wiki/F%C3%A9d%C3%A9ration_Tahitienne_de_Football" class="mw-redirect" title="Fédération Tahitienne de Football">Fédération Tahitienne de Football</a>.
</p>
<h4><span id="Va.27a"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Va'a">Va'a</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Va&#039;a">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The Polynesian traditional sport <a href="/wiki/Va%27a" title="Va&#39;a">va'a</a> is practiced in all the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> French Polynesia hosts the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Hawaiki_nui_va%27a&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Hawaiki nui va&#39;a (page does not exist)">Hawaiki nui va'a</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaiki_nui_va%27a" class="extiw" title="fr:Hawaiki nui va&#39;a">fr</a>&#93;</span> an international race between Tahiti, Huahine and Bora Bora.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Surfing">Surfing</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Surfing">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>French Polynesia is famous for its <a href="/wiki/Surf_break" title="Surf break">reef break</a> waves. <a href="/wiki/Teahupo%27o" title="Teahupo&#39;o">Teahupo'o</a> is probably the most renowned, regularly ranked in the best waves of the world.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> This site hosts the annual <a href="/wiki/Billabong_Pro_Teahupoo" title="Billabong Pro Teahupoo">Billabong Pro Tahiti</a> surf competition, the 7th stop of the <a href="/wiki/World_championship_tour_(WCT)_surfing" class="mw-redirect" title="World championship tour (WCT) surfing">World Championship Tour</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> and is scheduled to host the surfing events of the <a href="/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics" title="2024 Summer Olympics">2024 Summer Olympics</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Kitesurfing">Kitesurfing</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Kitesurfing">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>There are many spots to practice kitesurfing in French Polynesia, with Tahiti, Moorea, Bora-Bora, Maupiti and Raivavae being among the most iconic.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg/220px-Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg/330px-Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg/440px-Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="563" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Fakarava_Atoll_Passe_Sud.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Fakarava atoll, south pass</div></div></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Diving">Diving</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Diving">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>French Polynesia is internationally known for diving. Each archipelago offers opportunities for divers. <a href="/wiki/Rangiroa" title="Rangiroa">Rangiroa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fakarava" title="Fakarava">Fakarava</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Tuamotus" title="Tuamotus">Tuamotu</a> islands are the most famous spots in the area.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Rugby">Rugby</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Rugby">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Rugby_union_in_French_Polynesia" title="Rugby union in French Polynesia">Rugby union in French Polynesia</a></div>
<p>Rugby is also popular in French Polynesia, specifically Rugby union.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Television">Television</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Television">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Television channels with local programming include <a href="/wiki/Polyn%C3%A9sie_la_1%C3%A8re" title="Polynésie la 1ère">Polynésie la 1ère</a> (established in 1965) and <a href="/wiki/Tahiti_Nui_Television" title="Tahiti Nui Television">Tahiti Nui Television</a> (established in 2000). Channels from metropolitan France are also available.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy_and_infrastructure">Economy and infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Economy and infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_French_Polynesia" title="Economy of French Polynesia">Economy of French Polynesia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:French_Polynesia_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of French Polynesia exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_(8076090305).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_%288076090305%29.jpg/220px-DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_%288076090305%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_%288076090305%29.jpg/330px-DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_%288076090305%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_%288076090305%29.jpg/440px-DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_%288076090305%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4272" data-file-height="2848" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:DSC00033_Polyn%C3%A9sia_Mor%C3%A9a_island_Haapiti_Beach_(8076090305).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tourism is an important source of income for French Polynesia.</div></div></div>
<p>The legal tender of French Polynesia is the <a href="/wiki/CFP_franc" title="CFP franc">CFP franc</a> which has a fixed exchange rate with the <a href="/wiki/Euro" title="Euro">euro</a>. The nominal <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">gross domestic product</a> (or GDP) of French Polynesia in 2019 was 6.01&#160;billion <a href="/wiki/United_States_dollar" title="United States dollar">U.S. dollars</a> at market exchange rates, the seventh-largest economy in Oceania after <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hawaii" title="Hawaii">Hawaii</a>, <a href="/wiki/Papua_New_Guinea" title="Papua New Guinea">Papua New Guinea</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Guam" title="Guam">Guam</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-8">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The GDP per capita was US$21,615 in 2019 (at market exchange rates, not at <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">PPP</a>), lower than in Hawaii, Australia, New Zealand, Guam, and New Caledonia, but higher than in all other independent insular states and dependent territories of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_8-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-8">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>French Polynesia was severely affected by the <a href="/wiki/Financial_crisis_of_2007%E2%80%932008" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial crisis of 2007–2008">Global Financial Crisis of 2008</a> and subsequent <a href="/wiki/Great_Recession" title="Great Recession">Great Recession</a>, and experienced as a result 4 years of <a href="/wiki/Recession" title="Recession">recession</a> from 2009 to 2012. French Polynesia renewed with economic growth in 2013, and experienced strong economic growth in the 2nd half of the 2010s, with an average <a href="/wiki/Real_gross_domestic_product" title="Real gross domestic product">real GDP</a> growth rate of +2.8% per year from 2016 to 2019, before being affected by the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 pandemic</a> in 2020, which has led to another recession.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>French Polynesia has a moderately developed economy, which is dependent on imported goods, tourism, and the financial assistance of mainland France. Tourist facilities are well developed and are available on the major islands. Main agricultural productions are <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconuts</a> (<a href="/wiki/Copra" title="Copra">copra</a>), vegetables and fruits. French Polynesia exports <a href="/wiki/Noni" class="mw-redirect" title="Noni">noni</a> juice, a high quality <a href="/wiki/Vanilla_production_in_French_Polynesia" title="Vanilla production in French Polynesia">vanilla</a>, and the famous <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_pearl" title="Tahitian pearl">black Tahitian pearls</a> which accounted for 55% of exports (in value) in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-FT_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FT-76">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>French Polynesia's seafloor contains rich deposits of <a href="/wiki/Nickel" title="Nickel">nickel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cobalt" title="Cobalt">cobalt</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manganese" title="Manganese">manganese</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Copper_extraction" title="Copper extraction">copper</a> that are not exploited.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2008, French Polynesia's imports amounted to 2.2&#160;billion U.S. dollars and exports amounted to 0.2&#160;billion U.S. dollars.<sup id="cite_ref-FT_76-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FT-76">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Transportation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transportation_in_French_Polynesia" class="mw-redirect" title="Transportation in French Polynesia">Transportation in French Polynesia</a></div>
<p>There are 53 airports in French Polynesia; 46 are paved.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-22">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Fa%27a%27%C4%81_International_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Fa&#39;a&#39;ā International Airport">Fa'a'ā International Airport</a> is the only international airport in French Polynesia. Each island has its own airport that serves flights to other islands. <a href="/wiki/Air_Tahiti" title="Air Tahiti">Air Tahiti</a> is the main airline that flies around the islands.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Communication">Communication</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Communication">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2017, Alcatel Submarine Networks, a unit of <a href="/wiki/Nokia" title="Nokia">Nokia</a>, launched a project to connect many of the islands in French Polynesia with underwater fiber optic cable. The project, called NATITUA, is intended to improve French Polynesian broadband connectivity by linking Tahiti to 10 islands in the Tuamotu and Marquesas archipelagos.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> In August 2018, a celebration was held to commemorate the arrival of a submarine cable from <a href="/wiki/Papeete" title="Papeete">Papeete</a> to the atoll of Hao, extending the network by about 1000 kilometres.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_people">Notable people</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=French_Polynesia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Notable people">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Parau_api,_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Parau_api%2C_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg/220px-Parau_api%2C_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="164" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Parau_api%2C_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg/330px-Parau_api%2C_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Parau_api%2C_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg/440px-Parau_api%2C_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4746" data-file-height="3531" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Parau_api,_by_Paul_Gauguin.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Painting of <i><a href="/wiki/Tahitian_Women_on_the_Beach" title="Tahitian Women on the Beach">Two Women of Tahiti</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Paul_Gauguin" title="Paul Gauguin">Paul Gauguin</a></div></div></div>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Ta%C3%AFna_Barioz" title="Taïna Barioz">Taïna Barioz</a> (born 1988), <a href="/wiki/FIS_Alpine_World_Ski_Championships_2011" title="FIS Alpine World Ski Championships 2011">World Champion</a> skier representing France.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Billy_Besson" title="Billy Besson">Billy Besson</a>, Olympic sailor representing France</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Michel_Bourez" title="Michel Bourez">Michel Bourez</a> (born 1985), professional surfer.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Cheyenne_Brando" title="Cheyenne Brando">Cheyenne Brando</a> (1970–1995), model, daughter of <a href="/wiki/Marlon_Brando" title="Marlon Brando">Marlon Brando</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tarita_Teriipaia" title="Tarita Teriipaia">Tarita Teriipaia</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jacques_Brel" title="Jacques Brel">Jacques Brel</a> (1929–1978), Belgian musician who lived in French Polynesia near the end of his life.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jean_Gabilou" title="Jean Gabilou">Jean Gabilou</a>, singer (born 1944), represented France in the <a href="/wiki/1981_Eurovision_Song_Contest" class="mw-redirect" title="1981 Eurovision Song Contest">1981 Eurovision Song Contest</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Chantal_Galenon" title="Chantal Galenon">Chantal Galenon</a>, politician and women's rights activist.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Paul_Gauguin" title="Paul Gauguin">Paul Gauguin</a> (1848–1903), French <a href="/wiki/Post-impressionist" class="mw-redirect" title="Post-impressionist">post-impressionist</a> painter who spent the last years of his life in French Polynesia.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Conrad_Hall" title="Conrad Hall">Conrad Hall</a> (1926–2003), American cinematographer.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Vaitiare_Bandera" title="Vaitiare Bandera">Vaitiare Hirson-Asars</a> (born 1964), actress.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ella_Koon" title="Ella Koon">Ella Koon</a> (born 1979), singer, actress and model.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Karina_Lombard" title="Karina Lombard">Karina Lombard</a> (born 1969), French-American model and actress.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pouvanaa_a_Oopa" title="Pouvanaa a Oopa">Pouvāna'a 'Ō'opa</a> (1895–1977), politician and Tahitian nationalist.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fabrice_Santoro" title="Fabrice Santoro">Fabrice Santoro</a> (born 1972), professional tennis player.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tarita_Teriipaia" title="Tarita Teriipaia">Tarita Teriipaia</a> (born 1941), actress, third wife of Marlon Brando.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marama_Vahirua" title="Marama Vahirua">Marama Vahirua</a> (born 1980), footballer, cousin of Pascal Vahirua.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pascal_Vahirua" title="Pascal Vahirua">Pascal Vahirua</a> (born 1966), French former international footballer.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/C%C3%A9lestine_Hitiura_Vaite" title="Célestine Hitiura Vaite">Célestine Hitiura Vaite</a> (born 1966), writer.</li></ul>
